How APPSC makes a statement true or false — full APPSC report

16614 APPSC past-paper questions (every APPSC paper in the app: Group 1, Group 2, departmental and other recruitments) taken apart unit by unit: 68101 units (20608 true, 47493 false; 2276 of the false ones are the wrong pairs that match-question distractors offer).

Rulebook in one page (16,614 APPSC PYQs)

  1. Most false statements are made by swapping one fact, not by changing the language. Top changes: number / quantity changed (6759), person swapped (5736), wrong category / class (5735), place swapped (5668), date / year shifted (3311), organisation / scheme / body swapped (2590). For every statement ask: which number, name, place, date or body here could have been swapped?
  2. Words that point to FALSE (share of statements carrying them that were false; base rate 36%): absolute 67% · negation 64% · agent 46% · causal 44%
  3. Words that lean TRUE: hedge 5% false
  4. Multi-statement questions (486): none false 102, one false 224, two false 114, three+ 46. False statement position: #1 151, #2 205, #3 139, #4 85, #5 12. Anchoring on the surest statement reached the key in 436 of 486.
  5. Match (410): one sure pair / a unique item solved 370 of 410.
  6. Assertion–reason (133): outcomes A✓ R✓ explains 64, A✓ R✓ not-explain 27, A✓ R✗ 24, A✗ R✓ 18 – never assume 'both true, R explains A'.
  7. NOT / except: turn it into 'which three are true' – the true options share a pattern, the false one breaks it.
  8. Sequence (62): fixing the first/last item solved 52.
  9. One-liners (14525): 10667 (73%) are pure recall – knowledge decides; odd-one-out and same-class checks rescue the rest.

2. English rules — how often a statement carrying the cue is false

Measured on the numbered statements, assertion/reason parts, and sequence steps (2,201 units), where a statement can freely be true or false. Base rate: 36% false. A rule well above the base rate is a warning sign; well below leans true. (One-liner options are shown separately below, because 3 of 4 options are false by design.)

4. Subject habits (examples from the false statements)

History

  • History(AP reform): journal attributed to a contemporary reformer
  • Polity: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 - key dates and numbers
  • Culture: author-to-text pairing; Satavahana court writers offered together
  • History: movement-leader pairing among Guntur 1921-22 campaigns
  • History(AP reform): reform work attributed to a contemporary leader
  • History(AP reform): event date shifted to a nearby year/day
  • History(AP): dates/numbers of the fasts (Sitaram 1951, Sriramulu 1952) and Wanchoo report
  • Polity: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 - section number to provision
  • History(AP): Mahasabha session-president pairs; Assembly passed vs rejected
  • AP History: Visalandhra movement - who/what/where of the campaign

Polity

  • Polity: borrowed feature attributed to the wrong source country
  • Polity: Fundamental Rights - article number swapped
  • Polity: borrowed feature attributed to wrong country
  • Polity: Constituent Assembly roles (President / adviser / drafting chair / member) swapped
  • Polity: provision moved to an earlier/later Act in the constitutional chain
  • Polity: number nominated by the President to the RS (Art. 80)
  • Polity: writ name paired with the wrong meaning
  • Polity: President's tenure dates swapped among successive Presidents
  • AP: section numbers of AP Reorganisation Act 2014
  • Polity: presiding officer of joint sitting - RS Chairman / President / ministers as traps

Economy

  • Economy: AP budget figure - distractors are near-by numbers
  • Economy: Union Budget figure with a near-identical distractor series
  • Polity/AP: section number of APRA 2014 swapped with a neighbouring section
  • Economy (AP): sector share figure with neighbouring years/sectors as distractors
  • Economy: plan period dates
  • Economy: scheme matched to plan
  • Economy: number or date of the 1969 nationalisation shifted
  • Polity/AP: section-number of the AP Reorganisation Act swapped for a neighbouring one
  • Economy: first/ex-officio chairman of a planning body swapped with another leader
  • Economy: pre-independence plan credited to the wrong author

Geography

  • Economy: mineral matched to its leading district
  • DM: location of a national institute swapped with other cities
  • Geography: synonym/property of black soil - other soils as decoys
  • Geography: city/region mapped to the wrong seismic zone
  • Geography: the most extensive soil of AP - other soil types as decoys
  • Disaster: flood facts and mitigation in India
  • AP geography: superlative area of a district
  • Geography: coastline length number, revised in 2025
  • Science: instrument-quantity pair (hydro- vs hygro- look-alike)
  • Geography: national park placed in the wrong State

Sci-Tech-Env

  • Economy/Intl: headline count of a UN framework (17 SDGs) vs near numbers
  • DM: Bhopal 1984 MIC leak - year/gas/type recall
  • Biology: vitamin-deficiency disease pairs swapped
  • Environment: numbers of the SDG framework
  • DM: NDMA vulnerability statistics (27/36 States-UTs, 5700 km coast, 68% drought, 58.6% quake)
  • Environment: the three pillars of sustainable development
  • S&T: acronym expansion with one word swapped
  • Energy: renewable vs non-renewable classification
  • Current affairs: Swachh Survekshan rank
  • Nutrition: deficiency-disease pairing

Current Affairs

  • IR/CA: summit or conference venue swapped with another host
  • Days: UN/international day - adjacent dates or other days' dates as distractors
  • IR: Republic Day chief guests of neighbouring years swapped
  • Sports: champion swapped with beaten finalist
  • Index: rank number changed
  • Sports: runner-up offered as winner
  • Pageants: runners-up offered for the winner
  • Sports: athlete-to-sport pairing
  • Sports: winner swapped with runner-up
  • Days: day linked to the birthday of its namesake

5. Sample question breakdowns

one-liner · History · APPSC Group-I 1995

Author of 'Kuvalayamala'?

(1) Baanudu · (2) Kundakunda · (3) Dandi · (4) Uddyotana Suri — key (4)

  • F a: Kuvalayamala was written by Baanudu
    → true fact: Kuvalayamala was written by Uddyotana Suri (779 CE). · change PERSON · cue: Baanudu
  • F b: Kuvalayamala was written by Kundakunda
    → true fact: Kundakunda wrote Samayasara. · change PERSON · cue: Kundakunda
  • F c: Kuvalayamala was written by Dandi
    → true fact: Dandi wrote Dasakumaracharita. · change PERSON · cue: Dandi
  • T d: Kuvalayamala was written by Uddyotana Suri
    · cue: Uddyotana Suri, Kuvalayamala

🔑 RECALL: Only Suri-named author is a Jain acharya of the 8th c.; recall Kundakunda=Samayasara, Dandi=Dasakumaracharita to eliminate.
Minimum knowledge: Kuvalayamala = Uddyotana Suri. · hard

one-liner · History · APPSC Group-I 2017

The earliest inscriptions found in India were written in

(1) Sanskrit in Brahmi script · (2) Prakrit in Brahmi script · (3) Tamil in Kharosthi script · (4) Pali in Kharosthi script — key (2)

  • F a: The earliest inscriptions found in India were written in Sanskrit in Brahmi script
    → true fact: Ashoka's edicts are in Prakrit, not Sanskrit. · change CATEGORY · cue: Sanskrit · rule: CONJUNCTION
  • T b: The earliest inscriptions found in India were written in Prakrit in Brahmi script
    · cue: Prakrit, Brahmi · rule: CONJUNCTION, SUPERLATIVE
  • F c: The earliest inscriptions found in India were written in Tamil in Kharosthi script
    → true fact: Earliest inscriptions are Prakrit in Brahmi; Kharosthi only in NW, Tamil-Brahmi later. · change PARTIAL · cue: Tamil, Kharosthi · rule: CONJUNCTION
  • F d: The earliest inscriptions found in India were written in Pali in Kharosthi script
    → true fact: Ashokan edicts are Prakrit, mostly in Brahmi; Kharosthi only in the north-west. · change PARTIAL · cue: Pali, Kharosthi · rule: CONJUNCTION

🔑 STMT_ANCHOR, CONTAINMENT: Fix the script as Brahmi (kills c, d), then language Prakrit not Sanskrit (kills a).
Minimum knowledge: Ashokan edicts: Prakrit language, Brahmi script. · easy

match · History · APPSC Group-II 2011

The earlist inscription on stone were in which language?

(1) Sanskrit · (2) Brahmi · (3) Pali · (4) Prakritha — key (4)

  • F a: The earliest inscriptions on stone were in the Sanskrit language
    → true fact: Earliest stone inscriptions (Ashoka) are in Prakrit. · change CATEGORY · cue: Sanskrit
  • F b: The earliest inscriptions on stone were in the Brahmi language
    → true fact: Brahmi is a script, not a language. · change CATEGORY · cue: Brahmi, language · rule: DEFINITIONAL
  • F c: The earliest inscriptions on stone were in the Pali language
    → true fact: Ashokan edicts are in Prakrit, not Pali. · change CATEGORY · cue: Pali
  • T d: The earliest inscriptions on stone were in the Prakritha language
    · cue: Prakritha, earliest · rule: SUPERLATIVE

🔑 SAME_CLASS, RECALL: Reject Brahmi as it is a script; of the languages, Ashoka's edicts are Prakrit.
Minimum knowledge: Ashokan edicts are in Prakrit. · easy

one-liner · History · APPSC Forest Beat Officer & Assistant Beat Officer (Mains) P

Who was the foreign ambassador who visited Mauryan court and provided detailed accounts of its administration, economy and urban life?

(1) Megasthenes · (2) Fa-Hien · (3) Al-Masaudi · (4) Marco Polo — key (1)

  • T a: Megasthenes was the foreign ambassador at the Mauryan court who described its administration
    · cue: ambassador, Mauryan · rule: AGENT
  • F b: Fa-Hien was the foreign ambassador at the Mauryan court who described its administration
    → true fact: Fa-Hien came in Gupta times (Chandragupta II). · change CHRONO · cue: Fa-Hien
  • F c: Al-Masaudi was the foreign ambassador at the Mauryan court who described its administration
    → true fact: Al-Masudi was a 10th-c. Arab traveller. · change CHRONO · cue: Al-Masaudi
  • F d: Marco Polo was the foreign ambassador at the Mauryan court who described its administration
    → true fact: Marco Polo visited in the 13th c. · change CHRONO · cue: Marco Polo

🔑 RECALL, ODD_ONE_OUT: Only an 'ambassador' fits: Megasthenes was Seleucus's envoy; others were travellers of later ages.
Minimum knowledge: Megasthenes = envoy at Chandragupta Maurya's court. · easy

match · History · APPSC Group-I 2019 (26-05-2019)

Which of the following pairs is NOT correctly matched?
(Traveller) - (Reigning Ruler)

(1) Fa-Hien - Chandragupta II · (2) I-Tsing (Yijing) - Ashoka · (3) Al-Beruni - Mahmud of Ghazni · (4) Ibn Battuta - Muhammad bin Tughluq — key (2)

  • T a: Fa-Hien - Chandragupta II
    · cue: Fa-Hien
  • F b: I-Tsing - Ashoka
    → true fact: I-Tsing came in 671-695 CE, a millennium after Ashoka. · change CHRONO · cue: I-Tsing, Ashoka
  • T c: Al-Beruni - Mahmud of Ghazni
    · cue: Al-Beruni
  • T d: Ibn Battuta - Muhammad bin Tughluq
    · cue: Ibn Battuta

🔑 MATCH_ANCHOR, NOT_REVERSE: Three pairs are textbook; I-Tsing is 7th c. CE while Ashoka is 3rd c. BCE, an obvious chronological gap.
Minimum knowledge: I-Tsing visited in the late 7th c. CE. · easy

match · History · APPSC AP History PYQ

Identify the incorrectly matched pair.

(1) Gautami Balashri - Nasik Inscription · (2) Kharavela - Hathigumpha Inscription · (3) Rudradaman - Nanaghat Inscription · (4) Ashoka - Maski Inscription — key (3)

  • T a: Gautami Balashri - Nasik Inscription
    · cue: Nasik
  • T b: Kharavela - Hathigumpha Inscription
    · cue: Hathigumpha
  • F c: Rudradaman - Nanaghat Inscription
    → true fact: Rudradaman's record is Junagadh; Nanaghat is Naganika's. · change MISPAIR · cue: Rudradaman, Nanaghat
  • T d: Ashoka - Maski Inscription
    · cue: Maski

🔑 MATCH_ANCHOR, NOT_REVERSE: Rudradaman (Saka) is known for Junagadh; Nanaghat is a Satavahana record, so (c) is wrong.
Minimum knowledge: Rudradaman = Junagadh; Nanaghat = Naganika. · medium

one-liner · History · APPSC Group-II 2008

What is the meaning of the word "Gulmika" mentioned in the Maski (Macedonian) inscription?

(1) Village headman · (2) A slave · (3) Trade tariff · (4) Landlord — key (1)

  • T a: 'Gulmika' in the inscription means Village headman
    · cue: Gulmika, headman · rule: DEFINITIONAL
  • F b: 'Gulmika' in the inscription means A slave
    → true fact: Gulmika was an officer over a group of villages. · change DEFINITION · cue: slave
  • F c: 'Gulmika' in the inscription means Trade tariff
    → true fact: Gulmika was an officer, not a tax. · change CATEGORY · cue: tariff
  • F d: 'Gulmika' in the inscription means Landlord
    → true fact: Gulmika was an officer/village head, not a landlord. · change DEFINITION · cue: Landlord

🔑 RECALL, SAME_CLASS: Recall gulma = troop/village group; the officer over it is a headman. Tariff is a different class (not a person).
Minimum knowledge: Gulmika = village-group head. · medium

NOT · History · APPSC Group-II Mains 2017 (15-07-2017)

The early History of the Satavahana family is not stated in the following work?

(1) Pratishtana Pura Kalpa · (2) Dwatrimsatupttalika · (3) Kadhasaritsagaram · (4) Gadhasapthasati — key (4)

  • T a: Pratishtana Pura Kalpa states the early history of the Satavahana family
    · cue: Pratishtana
  • T b: Dwatrimsatputtalika states the early history of the Satavahana family
    · cue: Dwatrimsat
  • T c: Kathasaritsagara states the early history of the Satavahana family
    · cue: Kathasaritsagara
  • F d: Gathasaptasati states the early history of the Satavahana family
    → true fact: Gathasaptasati is Hala's Prakrit verse anthology, not a history. · change CATEGORY · cue: Gathasaptasati, not · rule: NEGATION

🔑 NOT_REVERSE, ODD_ONE_OUT: Three are story/legend collections; Gathasaptasati is a love-verse anthology, the odd one.
Minimum knowledge: Gathasaptasati is a verse anthology. · medium

statements · History · APPSC Group-2 PYQ

Which of the following statements is/are 42. Ma incorrect?
a. Billa Surgam Palaeolithic cave site is located in Anantapuramu district.
b. Belum caves located in Nandyal district is the second longest cave in India.
c. Cave sites of Kurnool and Muchatla Chintamani Gavi in A.P. are the only places in India where tools made of animal bones were found in upper palaeolithic context.

(1) a and b · (2) b and c · (3) a only · (4) c only CO Gr a. C. G i. iii. iv. V — key (3)

  • F a: Billa Surgam Palaeolithic cave site is located in Anantapuramu district
    → true fact: Billa Surgam is in Kurnool (now Nandyal) district. · change PLACE · cue: Anantapuramu
  • T b: Belum caves in Nandyal district is the second longest cave in India
    · cue: second longest · rule: SUPERLATIVE
  • T c: Kurnool caves and Muchatla Chintamani Gavi are the only places in India with Upper Palaeolithic bone tools
    · cue: only · rule: ABSOLUTE

🔑 STMT_ANCHOR: Know Billa Surgam is in Kurnool, so (a) is incorrect: kills (b and c) and (c only); Belum being 2nd longest is well known, killing (a and b).
Minimum knowledge: Billa Surgam is in Kurnool. · medium

assertion · History · APPSC Assistant Tribal Welfare Officer (A.P. Tribal Welfare

The following statements contain an assertion and a reason. Read the statements carefully and answer from the given options.
Assertion (A): There is no evidence of tools making out of animal bones in upper paleolithic context in Andhra Pradesh.
Reason (R): One of the cave sites in Kurnool has given evidence of 90 percent tools being made out of animal bones.

(1) A and R is True and R is the correct explanation of A · (2) A and R is True but R is not the correct explanation of A · (3) A is True but R is False · (4) A is False but R is True — key (4)

  • F A: There is no evidence of tools made of animal bones in upper palaeolithic context in Andhra Pradesh
    → true fact: Kurnool caves gave Upper Palaeolithic bone tools. · change NEGATION · cue: no evidence · rule: NEGATION, ABSOLUTE
  • T R: One of the cave sites in Kurnool has given evidence of 90 percent tools made of animal bones
    · cue: 90 percent · rule: QUANTIFIER
  • F R explains A: R explains A
    → true fact: R contradicts A, so it cannot explain it. · change REVERSAL · cue: no evidence · rule: CAUSAL

🔑 AR_CHECK: R itself states bone tools in Kurnool, which directly contradicts A; so A false, R true.
Minimum knowledge: Kurnool caves have bone tools. · easy

NOT · History · APPSC Group-2 PYQ

Which one of the following statements is incorrect with reference to megalithic burials in Andhra?

(1) Tirupati has the largest anthropomorphic burial site collection · (2) Many numbers of cyst burials were found in Vishakhapatnam District · (3) Sarcophagus burials are more in number in Chittoor and Anantapuramu districts · (4) Krishna and Chittoor districts contained many numbers of dolmen burials — key (3)

  • T a: Tirupati has the largest anthropomorphic burial site collection
    · cue: largest · rule: SUPERLATIVE
  • T b: Many cist burials were found in Visakhapatnam district
    · cue: many, Visakhapatnam · rule: QUANTIFIER
  • F c: Sarcophagus burials are more in number in Chittoor and Anantapuramu districts
    → true fact: Andhra sarcophagi are at Sankhavaram (Kurnool) and Nagarjunakonda. · change PLACE · cue: more in number, Chittoor and Anantapuramu · rule: COMPARATIVE
  • T d: Krishna and Chittoor districts contain many dolmen burials
    · cue: many · rule: QUANTIFIER

🔑 NOT_REVERSE, RECALL: Recall sarcophagus = Sankhavaram (Kurnool); its placement in Chittoor-Anantapuramu is the false one.
Minimum knowledge: Sarcophagus burial = Sankhavaram, Kurnool. · hard

assertion · History · APPSC Assistant Tribal Welfare Officer (A.P. Tribal Welfare

The following statements contains an assertion followed by a reason. Read the statements carefully and answer from the given below options.
Assertion (A): Utnur site has shown the evidence of animal domestication in Andhra Pradesh during Neolithic period
Reason (R): An evidence of wooden enclosure built many times and burning of cow dung repeatedly has been found.

(1) A and R is True and R is the correct explanation of A · (2) A and R is True but R is not the correct explanation of A · (3) A is True and R is False · (4) A is False but R is True — key (1)

  • T A: Utnur shows evidence of animal domestication in Andhra during the Neolithic period
    · cue: Utnur, domestication
  • T R: Evidence of a wooden enclosure built many times and repeated burning of cow dung was found
    · cue: cow dung, many times
  • T R explains A: R explains A
    · cue: cow dung, enclosure · rule: CAUSAL

🔑 AR_CHECK: Cow-dung ash in cattle pens is direct proof of cattle-keeping, so R explains A.
Minimum knowledge: Utnur ash mounds = burnt cow dung in cattle pens. · medium

assertion · History · APPSC Assistant Tribal Welfare Officer (A.P. Tribal Welfare

The following statements contains an assertion followed by a reason. Read the statements carefully and answer from the given below options.
Assertion (A): The Microliths in South Indian were mostly made of milky quartz during Mesolithic period.
Reason (R): Nagarjunakonda and Renigunta sites in Andhra Pradesh have given evidence of these microliths.

(1) A and R is True and R is the correct explanation of A · (2) A and R is True but R is not the correct explanation of A · (3) A is True but R is False · (4) A is False but R is True — key (1)

  • T A: Microliths in South India were mostly made of milky quartz in the Mesolithic period
    · cue: mostly, milky quartz · rule: QUANTIFIER
  • T R: Nagarjunakonda and Renigunta in AP have given evidence of these microliths
    · cue: Nagarjunakonda, Renigunta
  • T R explains A: R explains A
    · cue: evidence of these · rule: CAUSAL

🔑 AR_CHECK: Both true; key takes the findspots as the evidence for A.
Minimum knowledge: South Indian microliths are of quartz. · hard

NOT · History · APPSC Group-2 PYQ

Which of the following is not a Harappan site?

(1) Sutkagendo · (2) Surkotada · (3) Sohgaura · (4) Rakhigarhi — key (3)

  • T a: Sutkagendo is a Harappan site
    · cue: Sutkagendo
  • T b: Surkotada is a Harappan site
    · cue: Surkotada
  • F c: Sohgaura is a Harappan site
    → true fact: Sohgaura (Gorakhpur) is a Mauryan copper-plate site, not Harappan. · change CATEGORY · cue: Sohgaura
  • T d: Rakhigarhi is a Harappan site
    · cue: Rakhigarhi

🔑 NOT_REVERSE, ODD_ONE_OUT: Sutkagendor, Surkotada, Rakhigarhi are standard Harappan sites; the unfamiliar Mauryan Sohgaura is the odd one.
Minimum knowledge: Sohgaura is a Mauryan copper-plate site. · easy

statements · History · APPSC Group-1 PYQ

The late Harappan cultures are primarily Chalcolithic in which tools of stone and copper are used. 5. II. They do not show metal objects requiring complicated casting. III. The Chalcolithic people in the later Harappan phase lived in villages. IV. In the late Harappan phase at Bhagwanpura in Haryana, burnt bricks were not found.

(1) 1, II and IV · (2) I, II and III · (3) II, III and IV · (4) I, II, III and IV — key (2)

  • T I: The late Harappan cultures are primarily chalcolithic, using stone and copper tools
    · cue: primarily, stone and copper · rule: HEDGE
  • T II: They do not show metal objects requiring complicated casting
    · cue: do not, complicated casting · rule: NEGATION
  • T III: Chalcolithic people in the later Harappan phase lived in villages
    · cue: villages
  • F IV: In the late Harappan phase at Bhagwanpura in Haryana, burnt bricks were not found
    → true fact: Late Harappan houses at Bhagwanpura were of burnt brick. · change NEGATION · cue: were not found, Bhagwanpura · rule: NEGATION

🔑 STMT_ANCHOR: Knowing IV is false (Bhagwanpura has burnt bricks) leaves only B, the one option without IV.
Minimum knowledge: Bhagwanpura has burnt-brick houses. · medium

assertion · History · APPSC Assistant Tribal Welfare Officer in A.P. Tribal Welfar

    · cue: crucial archaeological source
  • F R: The coins of Satakarni depict only religious symbols and no portraits of rulers.

  • F R explains A: R is the correct explanation of A
    → true fact: R is false, so it cannot explain A. · change OTHER · cue: only · rule: ABSOLUTE

Economy (645)

  1. The vicious circle argument points out the supply side connection between · key: Productivity and Income → Saving and capital are also links in the supply-side circle; key follows the NextGen wording.
  2. India's underdevelopment is not mainly due to · key: working efficiency of the population → Low labour efficiency is often listed as a cause; option d is only wrong because it is worded neutrally.
  3. Indian economy is primarily? · key: Agricultural economy → Services now dominate GDP; the key rests on the employment share.
  4. If the production and distribution systems are in the hands of private individuals, it is called? · key: Capitalist economy → No printed key; key inferred from the definition.
  5. Indian Economy is net · key: Mixed → Stem misprinted ('Indian Economy is net').
  6. Economics as the study of relationship between unlimited wants and scarce resources, according to · key: Lionel Robbins → Correct answer (Lionel Robbins) was absent from the printed options; added as option 5.
  7. In 2017-18, what was the sectoral share of the services sector to the GDP at current prices? · key: 54.4 → Economic Survey 2017-18 gave 55.2% of GVA (AE); 54.4 is the nearest option, not an exact match.
  8. What is the approximate percentage share of the primary sector in India's National Income ? · key: About 20 → No printed option is correct; option e (About 20) was added by the editor; the question confuses output with employment.
  9. The share of mining and quarrying in GVA at base price 2011-12 was in 2016-17 · key: 3.0 percent → At current prices the share was about 2.4%; 3.0 holds only at constant prices.

  407. In Andhra Pradesh, the Kesoram Cements is located in the district of: · key: Karimnagar → Karimnagar/Basantnagar is now in Telangana (Peddapalli); valid only for undivided AP.
  408. The Eco-ship Rainbow Warrior came to Mumbai to launch Greenpeace India's campaign on the Alang shipbreaking yard. Which statements are NOT true: a) ch · key: a,f → Crew names unverifiable; key relied on for a and b.
  409. Which of the following countries has the most developed paper pulp and paper industry? · key: Canada → USA is the largest pulp producer; Canada and USA both defensible. Same question elsewhere keyed Mexico.
  410. In which of the following countries is the pulp and paper industry most developed? · key: Mexico → Key looks wrong: Mexico does not have the most developed pulp and paper industry; USA/Canada do (same Q keyed Canada in y ya90150a0e6d).
  411. What was the percentage of equity stake of the Government when Maruti Udyog Ltd was established? · key: 74 → At incorporation (Feb 1981) GoI held 100%; 74% applies after the 1982 Suzuki JV.
  412. Which of the following statements are correct about Visakhapatnam-Chennai Industrial Corridor Development Program (VCICDP)? I. It is partly funded by · key: Only I, II and IV are correct → ADB's original CDP listed four nodes (Visakhapatnam, Machilipatnam, Donakonda, Srikalahasti-Chittoor); III arguably true under that version.
  413. The Gatiman Express runs between · key: Delhi-Agra → Gatimaan extended to Jhansi in 2018; key still valid as Delhi-Agra is on its route.
  414. Which is the fastest train in India? · key: Gatiman Express from Delhi to Agra → Outdated: Vande Bharat now also runs at 160 km/h and is usually called fastest.
  415. JICA is providing financial aid to India in · key: Ahmedabad - Mumbai Bullet train Project → JICA also funds some metros, but not Hyderabad Metro; key safe.
  416. What is the approximate track length of South Central Railway (choose nearest figure)? · key: 6000 km → Outdated: AP divisions moved to South Coast Railway (June 2026), SCR now smaller.
  417. What is the length of the National Highway of India 44 (NH-44)? · key: 3745 km → MoRTH later gives ~4,112 km; key follows NCERT.
  418. Which is the longest road tunnel in India? · key: Chenani - Nashri Tunnel → Atal Tunnel is often called longest highway tunnel above 10,000 ft; key follows overall length.
  419. As per Central Organization for Railway Electrification, India, till 1 March 2024, how much of total broad-gauge railway network has electrified? · key: 94 → Outdated now (99.6%, May 2026); key valid for date asked.
  420. Which is the highest airport in the world? · key: Lhasa → Lhasa is not the world's highest airport (Daocheng Yading ~4,411 m); key is best of options.
  421. Which airport is located in the highest region in the world? · key: Lhasa → Lhasa is not the world's highest airport (Daocheng Yading ~4,411 m); key is best of options.
  422. In all, how many international airports are there in India? · key: 16 → Outdated: now ~33 international airports (2023).
  423. Which of the following is not included in Edgar Dale's Cone of Experience? · key: Television → Later editions (1954) include television; key rests on 1946 version.
  424. All India Radio was previously known as · key: Indian Broadcasting service → Exact old name was Indian State Broadcasting Service; option b is the nearest.
  425. Which of the following statements regarding India's position in the global telecommunications market, as of September 2024, is/are correct? 1. India r · key: 1 only → Source notes the printed key was 'Both 1 and 2'; the corrected key (1 only) is used here.
  426. Match the ports of Andhra Pradesh with their locations. Ports: A. Krishnapatnam Port B. Gangavaram Port C. Ramayapatnam Port D. Bhavanapadu Port Locat · key: A-i, B-iv, C-ii, D-iii → After 2022 reorganisation Ramayapatnam may fall in SPSR Nellore; key uses Prakasam.
  427. Which of the following statements about the airports in Andhra Pradesh is/are CORRECT? 1. Airports in Andhra Pradesh register Rs 730 crore losses in f · key: Only 1 and 2 → Key rests on the printed answer; the Tirupati profit claim could not be independently verified either way.
  428. How many National Highways does the state of Andhra Pradesh have? · key: 38 → Key dated: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 lists 55 NHs (8,744 km).
  429. In Andhra Pradesh the road length maintained by the Roads and Buildings Ministry of March 2006 is · key: 63639 K.M → Undivided-AP figure; residual State's R&B network is now 45,379 km.
  430. In December 2021, Union Minister for Road Transport and Highways Nitin Gadkari inaugurated and laid the foundation stone for ______ road projects in A · key: 41 → Figures could not be matched to a report (Oct 2020: 16 projects; Feb 2022: 51 projects).
  431. Visakhapatnam Port is protected from sea waves and cyclones by the · key: Dolphin's Nose → Yarada Hills is arguably also right (Dolphin's Nose is part of it); key takes the specific headland.
  432. As of financial year 2022, the Visakhapatnam port ranks ______ in terms of port traffic. · key: third → FY 2021-22 data put JNPA (~76 MT) ahead of Visakhapatnam (~69 MT), i.e. 4th; Vizag was 3rd in FY 2020-21.
  433. The construction of which major infrastructure project in the 19th century helped facilitate transportation and trade in Andhra Pradesh, particularly · key: Visakhapatnam Port → Visakhapatnam port was built 1927-33 (20th century); Machilipatnam/Kakinada were the 19th-century ports. Key looks wrong on the century.
  434. In which district of Andhra Pradesh, is the proposed port at Dugarajapatnam located? · key: SPSR Nellore → Since April 2022 Vakadu mandal is in Tirupati district.
  435. Which of the following is not an export item from India? · key: Milk and Milk products → India does export some dairy products; key relies on 'not a significant export'.
  436. What is the approximate total length of the coastal line of India, including Lakshadweep and Andaman and Nicobar Islands? · key: 7,517 km → Recounted in 2025 to 11,098.81 km (PIB Dec 2025); key correct for its time.
  437. The Chinese Company that established Special Economic Zone in Nellore District to manufacture shoes is · key: Apache → Apache is a Taiwanese group, reported as China-based; 'Chinese' is loose.
  438. During 1999-2006, Andhra Pradesh State occupied seventh place in India in terms of compound growth rate of · key: Export Sector → Unverified against a source; rests on the printed key.
  439. Which of the following cities is not included in the Tentative list of UNESCO World heritage sites? · key: Thirupathi → Ahmedabad (2017) and Jaipur (2019) have since been inscribed.
  440. How many UNESCO World Heritage Sites does India have as on July 2025? · key: 44 → Now 45 with Sarnath (July 2026).
  441. In which of the following States, the tourist center Leh is situated? · key: Jammu & Kashmir → Since 31 Oct 2019 Leh is in UT of Ladakh.
  442. Ahobilam is situated in which district? · key: Kurnool → Now in Nandyal district (April 2022).
  443. Lambasingi is known as the 'Kashmir of Andhra Pradesh'. Under which district does it fall? · key: Vishakhapatnam → Now in Alluri Sitharama Raju district.
  444. Match the following List-I with List-II List-I Buddhist Monasteries List-II - District a) Bavikonda Ancient Buddhist Monastery 1. Eluru b) Guntupalli · key: a-2, b-1, c-3 → Yaganti is a Shiva temple, not a Buddhist monastery (list heading loose).
  445. The apex body of APSRTC namely A.P. Travel and Tourism Development Corporation became independent in the year · key: 1981 → Records suggest re-registration around 1980; 1981 kept per key.
  446. Which of the following is currently the biggest Thermal Power Plant (with an installed capacity of 4,760 MW) in India? · key: Vindhyachal Thermal Power Station, Madhya Pradesh → Option D in the source carries stray text 'Answer: (B) ...' (data artefact).
  447. Rat-Hole mining means · key: Mining by the family members → Key is loose: rat-hole mining is defined by narrow horizontal tunnels, not by who mines; 'family members' is the APPSC key.
  448. Which state in India produces the largest amount of coal ? · key: Orissa → Answer depends on year; Jharkhand/Chhattisgarh/Odisha alternate as No.1 and none of the first two is offered.
  449. As of April 2025, which of the following power projects in Andhra Pradesh is the largest thermal power station by capacity? · key: Dr. Narla Tata Rao Thermal Power Station → Rayalaseema capacity in explanation (1,050 MW) is outdated; ranking unchanged.
  450. Trans-Siberian Railway runs between the stations in Russia from: · key: Moscow to Vladivostok → NCERT traces the route from St. Petersburg, which makes C defensible in some books.
  451. The New Silk Route connects the countries · key: China and Germany → England is arguable: a Yiwu-London freight service ran from 2017.
  452. What is the rank of Andhra Pradesh in terms of length of coast line amongst the coastal states of India? · key: Third → Printed key at exam time was 'Second' (974 km, old count); the data key 'Third' follows the Dec 2025 PIB revision.
  453. According to Indian Meteorological Department, a heat wave will not be considered in a hilly region, until the temperature exceeds: · key: 30°C → Labelled NOT but is a fill-in threshold question; the 'not' is in the stem wording.
  454. Which of the following are a result of depositional activity of the Himalayan rivers? I. River Terraces II. Braided Channels III. Alluvial Fans IV. Na · key: II, III and IV only → Printed key missing; some keys give all four (fill terraces are depositional). Labelled one-liner but statements form.
  455. The Earth's Sources function for natural resources includes water minerals, fish stocks & ______. · key: Fossil Fuels → Trees, herbs and animals are also resources; answer is textbook-specific.
  456. Arrange the following wild life sanctuaries in a chronological order according to the year of their legal recognition?. A. Rollapadu Wild Life Sanctua · key: C, A, B and D → Linked note is a source line only; years from the explanation.
  457. According to Indian Meteorological Department, a heat wave will not be considered in a hilly region, until the temperature exceeds: · key: 30°C → Pattern labelled NOT but it is a one-liner threshold question; keyed option is true.
  458. Match the following (shares of States in value of mineral production 202 State A. Odisha B. Madhya Pradesh Share 1. 17.34% 2. 2.96% 3. 4.36% 4.44.11% · key: A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3 → Stem is garbled (year truncated '202').
  459. Which is one of the long-term ecological and economic consequences of recurrent droughts? · key: Depletion of groundwater, land degradation and rural migrati → Pattern labelled 'sequence' but it is a one-liner.
  460. As per Census of India 2011, which of the following group of states and Union territories does not have scheduled caste population? · key: Nagaland, Lakshadweep, Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Arunacha → No printed option was correct; option 5 was added by the editor.   461. Which of the following statements about the airports in Andhra Pradesh is/are CORRECT? 1. Airports in Andhra Pradesh register Rs 730 crore losses in f · key: Only 1 and 2 → Linked notes do not cover this; truth of 3 taken from key only.
  462. Which of the following statement(s) is/are INCORRECT for the mineral Asbestos? I. Asbestos is known for its heat resistance capacity II. Andhra Prades · key: Only II → II was true until 2013-14 (AP the sole producer); key reflects later IMYB data.
  463. In which district of Andhra Pradesh, the Sri Venkateswara National Park is situated? · key: Chittoor → Post-2022 the park falls in Tirupati and Annamayya districts; Kadapa portion also partly true - key valid for 2022 framing.
  464. Consider the following statements: I. Silicon and oxygen are common elements in all types of feldspar and sodium, potassium, calcium, aluminium etc. a · key: Statement I only → Attached note (granite fossils) is unrelated.
  465. Which of the following is an example of a structural mitigation strategy for earthquake prevention? · key: Creating building codes and standards → Strictly, building codes are often listed as non-structural (regulatory); key treats them as structural.
  466. Match the given Physical features with their details: Physical features Details I. Anai Mudi a. highest peak in the Western Ghats II. Mahendragiri b. · key: I-a, II-b, III-d, IV-c → Mahendragiri is NCERT's Eastern Ghats high point; Jindhagada/Arma Konda are higher per other sources.

Sci-Tech-Env (432)

  1. The study of physical geography in the past geological ages is included in · key: Historical geography → Option C may be a misprint of 'palaeogeography', which would be the more exact answer.
  2. Which of the following is not a subjective method of studying behavior? · key: Observation → Interview is sometimes classed as objective in some texts; the key holds since observation is the clearest objective method.
  3. According to Freud, during which period super ego is developed? · key: Latency Period → Freud places superego formation at the resolution of the Oedipus complex (end of phallic stage); 'Phallic' is arguably correct.
  4. Carroll's model includes six elements with ...... output variable(s), ...... input variable(s) and ...... intermediate variable(s) in that order. · key: 1, 1, 4 → Classification of Carroll's five factors into input/intermediate varies between guides; relies on printed key.
  5. Who criticized the subject centered traditional curriculum? · key: Alfred North Whitehead → Dewey is equally (often more) associated with criticising the subject-centred curriculum; key is contestable.
  6. A small weight put on the hole of the pressure cooker increases the pressure of steam because? · key: Cooker becomes air tight → Option B is a partly valid physical reason (small area -> high pressure for small weight).
  7. Which of the following microscopes can image individual atoms? · key: Atomic Force microscope → High-resolution TEM can also resolve atoms, so the electron microscope option is arguably also correct.
  8. The power of a tractor is generally measured in the following units: · key: Horsepower → Kilowatt is also a correct power unit; the key relies on 'generally'.
  9. The working of rockets is based on the principle of · key: Newton's law → Conservation of momentum is equally correct; two options are valid.
  10. Why a bird standing on a high voltage cable does not get electric shock? · key: There is no potential difference between its legs → Pattern tagged NOT but stem's 'not' is in the situation; keyed option is the true reason.
  11. The new time-clock invented by American scientists which shows the most exact information is: · key: Atomic Clock → Mercury-ion optical clocks (NIST) were in news; option d is arguably also correct.
  12. If a table-fan rotating at 50 rpm is placed under an electric lamp (AC supply), it would appear to be · key: rotating slowly backwards → At 50 rpm a fan turns too slowly for strobing from 100 Hz flicker; the physics does not clearly give 'backwards'.
  13. The material used for electrical fuse wire should have C) · key: Low melting point → High resistivity is also a required property; two options defensible.
  14. A convex mirror is used to correct which of the following? · key: Long sight → Stem says 'convex mirror'; should be convex lens.
  15. Which of the following human body organs behaves like the endocrine as well as exocrine glands? · key: Pancreas → Testis is also sometimes called a mixed gland (testosterone + sperm), but standard key is pancreas.
  16. Which of the following is associated with Diabetes Mellitus? · key: High sugar level and low insulin level in blood → Type 2 diabetes can have high insulin (resistance); key follows type 1 textbook view.
  17. The following bone is not found in man · key: None of these (all are human bones) → Printed key was Astragalus (wrong: it is the human talus); option e added and keyed by the source.
  18. What is the angle of vision for a normal healthy individual? · key: 60 Degree → Actual visual field is ~150 deg per eye and ~180 deg binocular; 60 deg is only the nasal/superior limit. Key is shaky.
  19. Which of the following enzyme found in tears destroys bacteria? · key: Lysosome → Printed 'Lysosome' is an organelle; correct term is lysozyme.
  20. What does the following sign indicate? (a cross symbol) · key: First Aid → Image not available; a cross can also denote hospital or crossroad depending on design.
  21. Which of the following creatures are stone-deaf? · key: Snakes → Snakes can sense some low-frequency airborne sound; 'stone-deaf' is the textbook simplification.
  22. Common salt besides being used in kitchen can also be used as the raw material for making the following: i. Washing soda ii. Bleaching powder iii. Bak · key: i, ii and iii → Printed options lacked the correct code; option 5 (i, ii and iii) was added.
  23. Why is Sodium normally kept immersed in kerosene oil? · key: In open air Sodium explosively reacts with oxygen → Option b (moisture) is also a valid reason; key follows NCERT 'catches fire in air'.
  24. carats Gold contains by weight: · key: 8.4% of copper → Stem lost '22' in extraction; 2/24 = 8.33%, key 8.4% (100-91.6).
  25. Which of the following statements are NOT true about gold? a) soft metal b) high density, 2.5x iron c) pure gold ornaments cannot be made d) not easil · key: Only c and d → e says 25% copper; strictly 25% other metals (copper/silver).
  26. : Which of the following is a correct match of chemical compounds and their uses? Chemical Compound I. Analgesics II. Antibiotics III. Antiseptics · key: I.c II.a III.b Use a. To treat infections b. To prevent the → a vs b overlap (antibiotics also inhibit microbes), so II/III needs care.
  27. Washing soap produces a scum with hard water and not much foam because the hard water contains · key: dissolved inorganic salts → Tagged NOT but the stem is a plain one-liner; keyed option is the TRUE one.
  28. Bullet-proof vests are made from a fabric of · key: nylon → Kevlar (aramid) is the modern answer; nylon keyed as the only polyamide offered.
  29. Which of the following is an example of colloid? · key: Milk → Water in oil (emulsion) is also a colloid; key takes milk.
  30. The presence of this vitamin in feed is compulsory to prevent cage layer fatigue in Chickens. · key: All of these → Stem says 'vitamin' but Ca and P are minerals; key 'All of these'.
  31. The element not present in fertilisers is · key: hydrogen → Urea/ammonium fertilisers contain H atoms; key means 'nutrient element'.
  32. At what age does bamboo flower? · key: 30 years → Species-dependent; Melocanna baccifera ~48 years; PMF says 50-100 years.
  33. Maximum Photosynthesis occurs in: · key: Red light → Some texts say blue absorption is higher; key follows red.
  34. In plants, active cell division occurs in the following parts · key: Roots → Stem apex also has a meristem; key takes roots.
  35. Which of the following nuts has the highest potassium content per equal weight? · key: Almonds → Almonds and peanuts are close; source-dependent.
  36. Which of the following is matched INCORRECTLY? · key: Zinc - Helps to activate immune cells and properly regulate → Zinc genuinely activates immune cells (NIH/Harvard); option b's garbled wording is the likelier intended mismatch. Key looks wrong.
  37. Out of the below, which food is richer in Zinc? · key: Chicken → Per 100 g almonds/dry legumes can match or exceed chicken; key debatable.
  38. In which of the following is Vitamin B1 (Thiamine) available? · key: Rice grain husk → Sunflower seeds are actually rich in thiamine.
  39. Out of the below, which is richer in Vitamin D? · key: Orange → None of the four has natural vitamin D; orange only as fortified juice.
  40. Macrolytic anaemia is due to the deficiency of: · key: Folacin → Stem misprints 'Macrolytic' for 'Macrocytic'.
  41. Which of the following is/are a source of Vitamin K? · key: Nuts, eggs, liver of animals → Green leafy vegetables are the richest vitamin K source; option (c) is partly true, key (d) is the official APPSC 2004 key.
  42. In which season Vitamin A is required in higher quantities? · key: Winter → No nutrition authority (ICMR-NIN) gives a season-based vitamin A requirement; 'No seasonal dependence' is scientifically better. Key looks wrong.
  43. Which metal has the maximum use as pure metal? · key: Aluminium → Copper is also used largely as pure metal; the superlative is unclear and the key relies on the source book.
  44. Which type of chemical process is tarnishing of silver? · key: Corrosion → Tarnishing also oxidises silver (Ag -> Ag+), so 'Oxidation' is defensible; key picks the more specific 'Corrosion'.
  45. Percentage of sodium chloride present in normal saline is · key: 0.9 → Question had no printed key; key supplied from standard fact.
  46. Which of the following is used in titration · key: Burette → Pipette is also used in titration; burette is the defining apparatus.
  47. The head of a matchstick is generally made of · key: Phosphorous → Safety-match head is mainly potassium chlorate + antimony trisulphide; phosphorus is on the strip. Key reflects strike-anywhere matches.
  48. Which of the following can be prepared using Methyl Alcohol? · key: Perspex → Bakelite uses formaldehyde, itself made from methanol; key relies on direct esterification for Perspex.
  49. In humans, Milk Teeth generally fall off at what age? · key: Between 6 to 8 years → Shedding actually spans ~6-12; 6-8 is the conventional key.
  50. One of the following is not administered through rectum · key: Ecbolic → Misoprostol (an ecbolic) can be given rectally; key holds for usual practice.
  51. If a person closes one eye and looks with the other eye only, he would see? · key: A two dimensional world → Strictly, monocular cues still give some depth; key follows the textbook simplification.
  52. Which of the elements found in human body has functional requirement? · key: Arsenic → Disputed: arsenic's essentiality in humans is unproven; no option is an established essential element.
  53. A blood vessel which pumps the blood from the heart to the entire body · key: Artery → Stem says the vessel 'pumps'; strictly the heart pumps and arteries carry.
  54. Which of the following systems of the human body is responsible when a person sneezes? · key: Respiratory system → Explanation says a printed key read 'Immune system'; key used here is Respiratory system.
  55. Which of the following materials in normally used in a breath analyzer test to ascertain whether a person is drunk or not? · key: Potassium dichromate crystals → Tagged NOT but the stem is positive ('normally used'); keyed option treated as TRUE.
  56. What is the dominant acid in a 'bee sting'? · key: Formic acid → Strictly bee venom is acidic peptides (melittin); formic acid is the textbook key.
  57. Arrange the following solutions in the ascending order of pH value? A. Lemon juice B. Human blood C. Milk of Magnesia D. Sodium Hydroxide Solution · key: A, B, C, D → Tagged one-liner but is a sequence question.
  58. If Hydrochloric Acid is added to few pieces of Zinc in a beaker and a burning match stick is brought near the mouth of the beaker, then? · key: The gas inside the beaker catches fire → Tagged match but is a one-liner.
  59. During winter in cold regions, bottles of vinegar are found to freeze. This happens because · key: Pure ethanoic acid has a high melting point → Explanation says a printed key read 'low melting point', which is wrong; key here (high) matches NCERT. Strictly vinegar is dilute, the NCERT fact is about pure acid.
  60. Match the following From Table A to Table B - Uses of Acids as Food Table A: I. Butyric Acid II. Maleic Acid III. Stearic Acid IV. Oxalic Acid Table B · key: I-4, II-3, III-1, IV-2 → Stem misprints Malic as 'Maleic'.
  61. Match the given kitchen items with the acid present in them Kitchen items: I. Orange; II. Tomato; III. Tamarind Acid present: a. Oxalic acid; b. Tarta · key: I-c, II-a, III-b → Tomato's main acid is actually citric/malic; oxalic is the NCERT textbook pairing.
  62. Match the following items with the acids found in them. Natural source: I. Orange II. Tamarind III. Tomato Acid: a. Tartaric Acid b. Oxalic Acid c. Ci · key: I-c, II-a, III-b → Options 2 and 4 are printed identically; tomato-oxalic is the NCERT pairing.
  63. Which of the following acids are present in lemons? (i) Citric acid (ii) Tartaric acid (iii) Malic acid · key: Only (i) and (iii) → Tagged one-liner but is a statements-type question.
  64. The magnitude of root pressure would be minimum during · key: Summer → Explanation implies an earlier key 'Rainy season'; textbooks vary (some say winter, low absorption).
  65. Plants that complete their life cycle within one season are called · key: Seasonal plants → Usual term is 'annuals'; key uses 'seasonal plants'.
  66. By self-pollination, the seeds formed are · key: Weak → Self-pollination does not necessarily give weak seeds; key relies on inbreeding-depression generalisation.
  67. Plants in which the ovules are not enclosed by any ovary wall and remain exposed, both before and after fertilisation, are called ...... · key: Gymnosperms → Tagged NOT but it is a direct one-liner (the 'not' is inside the definition); keyed option is the true one.
  68. Which of the following matches of the sweetener and its explanation is correct? Sweetener: I. Ortho-sulphobenzimide (Saccharin) II. Aspartame III. Ali · key: I-c, II-a, III-b → Descriptors are loose (saccharin and alitame are both high-potency); key follows NCERT wording.
  69. What taste do starchy substances have? · key: Sweet → Pure starch is nearly tasteless; key 'sweet' holds only via salivary amylase.
  70. Honey is sweeter than cane sugar or corn syrup. Which one of the following carbon sugars is responsible for this? · key: Fructose → Levulose (option b) is a synonym of fructose, so two options are correct; keyed d.
  71. What is a change of position? · key: Motion → Displacement is strictly the change of position; key 'Motion' is the school definition.
  72. A boat filled with heavy stones is in a lake. If the stones are thrown into the lake, the level of water with respect to the boat would? · key: Decrease → Stem 'with respect to the boat' is ambiguous: lake level falls but the boat rises relative to water.
  73. Which of the following materials is not commonly used as sound-proof material? · key: Iron sheers → Plain glass is also a poor insulator; key assumes laminated/double glass. Option typo 'sheers'.
  74. If egg shell is carefully dissolved in acid and then immersed in salt water, then the shell? · key: Shrinks → Stem says 'shell' though the shell is dissolved; it means the egg/membrane.
  75. Sweating is an · key: Endothermic process → Strictly the evaporation of sweat (not secretion) is endothermic.
  76. In humans, which cells can perform anaerobic respiration during oxygen shortage? · key: Muscle cells → RBCs are in fact exclusively anaerobic (no mitochondria); 'Blood cells' is arguably also true.
  77. Which of the following is present in plant cells but not cells of animals? · key: Cell Wall → Not a true NOT question: keyed option is the TRUE claim.
  78. The gas used for artificial ripening of green fruit is · key: ethylene → Acetylene (calcium carbide) is in practice used for artificial ripening in India; key prefers ethylene.
  79. Why does open portion of a cut apple turn brown when exposed to air? · key: Due to oxidation of an enzyme present in apple → Strictly the enzyme catalyses oxidation of phenols; the enzyme itself is not oxidised.
  80. A statement is given followed by two conclusions. Select the correct response from the given options. Statement: Aschelminthes are called roundworms. · key: Only conclusion (i) is correct. → Conclusion (ii) is factually true (NCERT XI organ-system level); key treats it as not following. Fact-check reading gives 'both'.
  81. Which of the following features is NOT common in Chordates and non-chordates? · key: Bilaterally symmetrical → Flawed: triploblasty and coelom are also absent in many non-chordates (sponges, cnidaria, flatworms), and internal fertilisation is not universal; all options arguably fit.
  82. The beak of an insect eating bird would be? · key: Long and sharp → Many insectivores (swallows) have short wide beaks; key is the textbook simplification.
  83. Which dog-breed and region-of-origin pair is NOT correctly matched? · key: Chihuahua - China → Greyhound-Egypt is traditional but disputed.
  84. What is the primary purpose of a Wasp's sting? · key: To defend themselves or their colony → Solitary wasps use the sting mainly to paralyse prey (Britannica); key follows textbook view.
  85. Why chameleon changes its colours? · key: To protect itself from predators → Science: mainly social signalling and thermoregulation (Stuart-Fox & Moussalli 2008); option a is partly true.
  86. No. of lobes present in left lung of cow · key: 3 → Modern veterinary nomenclature gives 2 lobes for the left lung (cranial with two parts, caudal); option b could be defended.
  87. The electrical resistance of the insulators is · key: Infinitely High → 'High' is also correct for real insulators; key takes the ideal case.
  88. An electric fuse is used to prevent? · key: Heavy current → Overheating (a) is the mechanism of a fuse's action; key heavy current.
  89. Which of the following is not a primary colour? · key: Yellow → Yellow is a primary in the pigment (RYB) system; key follows light primaries.
